# Change Log — Master Speaking

October 2026 revision. Original manuscript retained. The source reconstruction applies the earlier repairs, restores the complete Unit 5 AI question, then applies exact-match reviewed edits and reader-facing learning guidance. Production commentary is kept outside the book.

New classroom guidance is authored supplementary text, not recovered manuscript content. Design and production changes are described in PUBLICATION-READINESS.md.

| 58 | How to Use · October classroom guidance | (new editorial guidance) | Prepare a few key words, speak in your own words, listen for your partner’s main point, and ask a follow-up question. After feedback, repeat part of the task and notice one improvement. Aim for clear, thoughtful communication rather than memorized answers or imitation of a particular accent. | Reader-facing learning guidance; authorship and production history recorded only in external reports. | new supplementary text |
| 59 | How to Use · October classroom guidance | (new editorial guidance) | For personal or sensitive questions, you may use a fictional example or discuss a comparable team or community situation. You do not need to disclose private information. Respect other perspectives and ask permission before recording anyone. | Reader-facing learning guidance; authorship and production history recorded only in external reports. | new supplementary text |
| 60 | How to Use · October classroom guidance | (new editorial guidance) | When presenting research, identify the author or organization, the source title, and the date or page where available. Explain what the evidence supports, distinguish facts from opinions, and acknowledge uncertainty. Do not invent statistics or references. | Reader-facing learning guidance; authorship and production history recorded only in external reports. | new supplementary text |

| 70 | U1 Ch1 · October editorial review | The next time you hesitate to speak English, remember one important idea: people are usually more interested in your message than your mistakes. Speak confidently, listen carefully, and continue learning from every conversation. Every great communicator was once a beginner | When you hesitate to speak English, start with the point you want to make. Use the words you know, listen carefully, and ask for clarification when you need it. You can work on accuracy while taking part. Let each conversation guide what you practise next. | Supply missing final punctuation; preserve the author’s closing encouragement. Full-rewrite pass: Rewrite the complete reading paragraph for natural progression, concrete explanation and balanced claims; preserve the concepts assessed by the exercises. | high (reviewed) |
| 71 | U3 Ch1 · October editorial review | Fortunately, emotional intelligence is not a fixed trait. Unlike IQ, it can be developed through conscious practice. Reflecting on one’s emotions, listening actively, seeking feedback, and responding thoughtfully rather than impulsively are all practical strategies for strengthening emotional intelligence. | You can practise these skills in small ways. Notice an emotion before naming someone else’s intention. Listen actively, ask for feedback, and pause before responding. Afterwards, consider what helped and what you might change. Understanding another perspective does not require agreeing with it, and managing your response does not mean accepting disrespect. | Remove the misleading contrast implying IQ is fixed; use direct, practical language without claiming a measured outcome. Whole-book rewrite: Recompose the full paragraph for a clearer argument, concrete explanation, qualified claims and alignment with the lesson questions. | high (reviewed) |
| 72 | U5 Ch1 · October editorial review | One of AI’s greatest strengths is its ability to process enormous amounts of information in a very short time. It can recognize patterns, analyze data, and perform repetitive tasks more quickly and accurately than humans. As a result, businesses, hospitals, schools, and governments are increasingly relying on AI to improve efficiency and solve complex problems. | AI can process large amounts of information, recognize patterns, and handle some repetitive tasks quickly. Its usefulness and accuracy depend on the task, the system, and the data. A tool that performs well in one setting may fail in another. Businesses, hospitals, schools, and governments therefore need more than a demonstration of speed; they need ways to check results and decide who is responsible for them. | Replace an unqualified claim of greater speed and accuracy with task-dependent language; avoid vague hype. Whole-book rewrite: Recompose the full paragraph for a clearer argument, concrete explanation, qualified claims and alignment with the lesson questions. | high (reviewed) |

| 190 | U1 Ch1 · October editorial review | Many people believe that communication means speaking well. In reality, communication is much broader than speaking. | 263 | U8 Ch1 · October editorial review | This does not mean that native-speaker varieties are disappearing. British, American, Australian and other varieties of English remain highly influential. | 264 | U8 Ch1 · October editorial review | There is also a darker side to the story.
